Let's consider using "self-normalizing" iterative algorithm:
Given a, compute m=max c. The LP direction g solves max sum g s.t. a*g ≤ m (componentwise). This gives a direction that if g sum >1, interpolating improves. This is like Bregman projection onto set {x: a*x≤m}. Maybe we can solve LP for larger n using high-dimensional active constraints but can be slow. However if n up to 1024, LP with active rows top ~200 okay. We can combine with softmax.

Could extend LP: Instead of fixed a*f? Our f normalized, constraints (a*g)_k ≤ current M. But to reduce M itself, if we find g with same M and larger sum, after normalization score decreases by sum g? Since new normalized h = g/sumg; a*h = M? Wait (a*h)=g/sumg, max ≤ M/sumg <M. This is a multiplicative update. This is exactly previous. We can solve this LP more often with more rows. It may be powerful. But LP may be slow for n=4096; still okay maybe. We can implement active set rows based on peaks of c. To allow finding g with sum>1, constraints only at active rows where c currently high. If non-active rows could violate after scaling? The algorithm ensures new max maybe? They used top rows not all; after rescale, non-active may increase? Need check. But it's heuristic.

A powerful approach: Use "entropy projection" or "iterative balancing" to equalize coefficients:
Given a, target M (current max). Want find a with same sum? Multiplicative update: For each coefficient k with weight w_k (e.g., normalized excess c_k/M), update a_i ← a_i * exp(-η w_i) where w_i =∑_k p_k a_{k-i}/M? This is like gradient. If we set p proportional to c_k^q, can equalize.

Algorithm `local_search_log(a0, n, time_limit)`:
- a=normalize(a0); w=log(a)+ maybe center.
- Compute c, M, cur.
- Adam state m,v for w (or simple momentum). Since step size adaptive, use momentum: v = β v - λ*grad_norm? Actually w update: w += step. Let grad_log = grad_logscore / scale. We want descend soft score.
- Use Adam: update w = w - lr*grad_log. But constrained gradient on log-sum is grad_constrained = grad_a - dot(a,grad_a)*? (already for log). If using log-softmax w, derivative of F wrt w_j = a_j(gradF_j - ∑ a_i gradF_i). Use that. gradF = 4n*g. So grad_log_j = a_j*(gradF_j - dot(a,gradF)). Compute. dot(a,gradF)=2n? Since ∑_j a_j g_j? g = shift(p,a); dot(a,g)=? It equals ∑_i a_i ∑_k p_k a_{k-i} =∑_k p_k ∑_i a_i a_{k-i}=∑_k p_k c_k = weighted average of c (around M). Times4n =4n avg. Fine.
- Softmax w for a each iteration: `a = np.exp(w - w.max()); a/=a.sum()`.
- Step size: Use Adam lr, start maybe 0.5. But grad_log scale ~a*gradF ~ (1/n)*(1/n)=1/n^2? Let's estimate: g_i ~ p_k a ~ (1/n)*(1/n)=1/n^2? Actually p normalized over 2n, a~1/n, so g~1/n^2; gradF=4n*g ~4/n; dot ~? grad_log ~a*4/n ~4/n^2. Very small. Adam normalizes by sqrt(v), so lr can be O(1). But changes in w may be too large. Try lr=0.1-1. For n=512, Adam normalized step maybe ~lr. Use beta. Accept if hard score improves.

To improve speed, use simple Stiefel: w -= alpha * normalized_gradient (L2=1) maybe. Adam easier.

- Temperature: p=softmax((c-M)/tau). Start tau_factor=0.25 (tau=0.25*M). Reduce over iterations to 0.01. As tau small, p concentrates on max coefficients.
- Acceptance: maintain current hard score cur. For each candidate a_new, compute c_new and max. If score_new < cur*(1-1e-5) or lower than best local, accept, reset no_improve. Else try smaller step. If no improve after X, increase tau or random perturb.

This is essentially smooth subgradient with softmax.

We can also implement "active percentile" weights: choose p_k = clip((c_k - M + delta)/delta,0,1) normalized, with delta = frac*M. This emphasizes peaks and avoids exponential underflow; similar to smooth_step but all lags. Use delta = temp_frac*M. For each iteration, delta decreases. p=positive part. This is stable and lower cost. Then grad_a=2*shift(p,a) (omitting constant). This is previous smooth_step with e=(c-M)/temp clipped, but previous only tried alphas and not iterative. We'll make iterative with backtracking and restarts.

Pseudo:
```
def local_cos(a0, budget, rng):
  a=normalize(a0); n=a.size
  c=conv(a); M=c.max(); cur=2*n*M
  w=log(a+eps)
  temp=0.25
  no=0
  deadline=...
  while time<deadline:
    c=conv(a); M=c.max(); cur=2*n*M
    delta=max(1e-3, temp*M)
    p=(c-M+delta)/delta; p[p<0]=0; p/=p.sum()
    g=shift(p,a)
    # direction on simplex: a - eta*g, then normalize? Or log update
    # Use log update: w -= eta * g/(a+eps)? Because a*exp(-eta*g/a) approx a - eta*g.
    # Multiplicative: a_new = normalize(a * exp(-eta * g / (a+floor))). This avoids zeros.
```
 Multiplicative update may be better: a_i ← a_i exp(-η * (g_i/a_i)) = a_i -η g_i first order. This is projected on log-sum. Step size η can be tuned. Since g_i ~1/n^2, g_i/a_i ~1/n. For n=512, ~0.002; η=10 changes 2%. Good. Use adaptive.

We can implement both log-Adam and multiplicative. Simpler multiplicative with backtracking:
```
def propose_from(a,eta):
    rate = g / np.maximum(a,1e-300)
    rate = np.clip(rate, -2, 2) # avoid overflow
    anew = a * np.exp(-eta*rate)
    anew = normalize(anew)
    return anew
```
But rate large where a small. Use g directly: `anew = normalize(a - eta*g*scale)` with nonnegative. Scale g to e.g. max=1, then step alpha relative to a: a_new = a - step * g/scale * maybe √? This is projected gradient. Let's choose direct projected gradient with step size in terms of a: `a_new = a - alpha * g / np.max(g)`; alpha in [0.01,0.5]. Since g values size ~a, step comparable to a*alpha. Normalize, clip. Backtrack.

For each iteration, try alpha list (0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5). Accept first that improves hard score. This is robust. Use p weighted peaks.

def local_search(a0, time_limit, rng, state):
    a = normalize(a0); n=a.size
    if n < 2: return
    c, M = conv_max(a)
    cur = 2.0*n*M
    best_local = cur; best_a=a.copy()
    update_global(state,best_a,best_local)
    # multiplicative/projected gradient
    no_improve=0
    temp_frac=0.35
    alpha=0.05
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       c=M
       # compute p
       delta = max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
       p = (c - M + delta) / delta
       if p.sum()==0: p[argmax c]=1
       p /= p.sum()
       g = shift_corr(p,a)
       # scale g
       gmax = max(g)
       if gmax<=0: break
       norm_g = g/gmax
       improved=False
       # try several step sizes around alpha
       for step in (alpha*4, alpha*2, alpha, alpha*0.5, alpha*0.25, alpha*0.125): # maybe decreasing
           b = a - step * norm_g * np.sqrt? maybe step*? ; b=np.maximum(b,0); normalize
           sc = score(b)
           if sc < cur - 1e-12:
               cur=sc; a=b; best=...; no=0; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          # try log-multiplicative
          for rate in ...:
             b = a * np.exp(-step * (norm_g / (a+1e-300)))? This can be huge.
          # if no, random perturb
       if not improved:
          no +=1
          if no>20:
             temp_frac = min(0.8, temp_frac*1.2)
             a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.05+0.1*no/...)
          else:
             # maybe alternate increasing step
             alpha = min(0.8, alpha*1.1)
       else:
          temp_frac = max(0.03, temp_frac*0.92)
          alpha = max(0.01, alpha*0.85)
```
The projected gradient `a - step*norm_g`: Since norm_g values order a? g_i ~? If norm_g ~1, step=0.05, subtract 0.05 from each a (~0.002 for n=512), huge, many zeros. Need scale norm_g to sum? Let's define direction d = g / np.sum(g) *? g sum = weighted avg c ~M. g_i ~M (same scale as a). To move a by a fraction, step should be O(0.1) times a? If d normalized sum1, then a - step*d moves sum by step, normalize. step=0.05 changes 5% of total mass, reasonable. So `d = g / g.sum()`; `b = a - step * d` with step in [0.01,0.2]. This is a projected gradient direction from peaks. But gradient of score wrt a is 2n g_i; direction should be -g_i, not normalized by sum. Normalizing by sum makes step relative to total mass; good.

However near zero, b may negative; clip. If many negative, after normalization maybe okay. Try steps. If no improve, try smaller.

The previous smooth_step used `h=f - alpha*scale*d` where d = r - mean(r), scale max. That centered gradient to preserve sum without normalization. Our `-g` and then normalize equivalent to subtracting mean? Let's use centered to avoid bias: `d = g - np.mean(g)` (or dot? Constraint sum fixed, subtract mean). Then `b = a - step * d / max|d|`; normalize. This corresponds to tangent gradient. Since g sum fixed, subtract mean preserves sum approx. Step relative. Good.

For peaks, g_i indicates how much mass a_i participates in peaks. We want reduce high g and increase low g. Centered direction does that. Use `scale = max(abs(d))`. step list [0.01,0.03,0.08,0.2,0.5,1.0]. This is similar to previous.

Constants:
`def _pow2_at_least(m): return 1 << (int(max(1,m)).bit_length())`

`def normalize(a)`:
- a=np.asarray(a,float)
- if a.size==0 return np.ones(1)
- a=np.maximum(a,0)
- s=a.sum()
- if not finite or s<1e-300: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
- return a/s

`def _fft_size(length)`:
 size=1
 while size<length: size <<=1
 return size

`def convolution(a)`:
 n=a.size
 size=_fft_size(2*n-1)
 fa=np.zeros(size); fa[:n]=a
 F=np.fft.rfft(fa)
 c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]
 return c

`def shift_correlation(p,a)`:
 n=len(a); m=len(p); need=n+m-1; size=_fft_size(need)
 fa= zeros; fa[:n]=a
 pb=zeros; pb[:m]=p[::-1]
 out=irfft(rfft(fa)*rfft(pb), size)
 idx=(m-1)-np.arange(n)
 return out[idx]
Need ensure out values positive real; small negative clip? `np.maximum(out[idx],0)`? Since p,a nonnegative, should be nonnegative. Use `np.asarray(..., dtype=float)`; set tiny negative 0.

`def score_normalized(a)`:
 a=normalize(a); c=convolution(a); m=c.max(); return 2.0*a.size*m

`def score_and_max(a)`: returns score, c, M. To avoid recompute.

`def gen_binomial(n, alpha=0.5)`:
 c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1.0
 for i in range(1,n): c[i]=c[i-1]*(i+alpha-1)/i
 c=np.maximum(c,0); return normalize(c)
For alpha small positive, factors positive. For alpha=0? factor (i-1)/i, ends 0 at i? But use alpha≥0.1.

`def gen_beta_power(n,a,b)`:
 x=np.linspace(1e-6,1-1e-6,n)
 y=x**a*(1-x)**b
 if a<-0.99 maybe use (i+0.5)/n.
 Use `x = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n`
 y = x**a * (1-x)**b, handle negative a by adding? x>0. For a=-0.5, x^(−0.5) fine. Clip.
 return normalize(y)

`def gen_random_smooth(n,rng, base=None)`:
 z=rng.normal(0,1,n); smooth with np.convolve length max(1,n//32); y=exp(0.7*z_smooth); if base: y=base + y*rng.random; normalize.

`def random_perturb(a,rng,sigma)`:
 b=a*np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,a.size)); return normalize(b)

`def update_global(state, seq, score)`:
 as previous.

`def local_search(a0, time_limit, rng, state)`:

 In each iteration:
   c, M = conv_max(a)
   cur=score
   if cur<state: update
   if M <=0: break
   # choose active weights p
   # cycle temp_frac maybe
   p = get_peak_weights(c, temp_frac)
   g = shift_correlation(p,a)
   # center direction
   d = g - g.mean()
   scale = max(np.abs(d))
   if scale<1e-300: random perturb
   improved=False
   # try additive

   if not improved:

           rate = np.clip(rate, -safety, safety)
           b = a * np.exp(-beta * rate)

   if improved:

          # maybe change temp

          no=0
   # time check: if no improves many, break to allow other tasks? But local_search runs until limit.
```

Initial portfolio function:
```
def make_initials(n, rng):
    seqs=[]
    # binomial and alpha variants
    for alpha in (0.5,0.55,0.45,0.6): seqs.append(gen_binomial(n,alpha))
    # generalized with shaping
    base=gen_binomial(n,0.5)
    idx=np.arange(n)
    for r in (-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5,1.0):
        y=base * ((idx+1)/(n+1))**r
        seqs.append(normalize(y))
    # beta powers
    for a in (-0.5,-0.3,-0.1,0.1,0.3):
       for b in (0.0,0.3,0.8): seqs.append(gen_beta_power(n,a,b))
    # random smooth from base
    for _ in range(4):
       noise=smooth_noise(n,rng)
       seqs.append(normalize(base + noise))
    # two-sided singular maybe
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    seqs.append(normalize(1/np.sqrt(x+1e-3) + 1/np.sqrt(1-x+1e-3)))
    return seqs
```
Need no too many for each n; for n=2048 and many sequences scoring cost okay. But local tasks limited.

Task scheduling:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=1800):
  rng=default_rng(seed)
  state=...
  hard_deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s-70
  # evaluate simple fallbacks
  update_global([1/3,2/3] score16/9)
  initial_n=[128,256,512,1024]
  candidate_lists=[]
  for n in [128,256,512,1024,2048]:
     if time > deadline: break
     seqs=make_initials(n,rng)
     scored=[]
     for seq in seqs:
        sc=compute_score(seq)
        update_global(state,seq,sc)
        scored.append((sc,seq))
     scored.sort()
     # keep top K for local search
     K = 3 if n<=256 else 2 if n<=1024 else 1
     for sc,seq in scored[:K]:
         candidate_lists.append((seq,sc,n))
     print...

Pseudo local_search optimized:
```
def local_search(a0, time_limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=25):
    a=normalize(a0); n=a.size
    if n<2 or time_limit<1: return
    c,M=conv_max(a); cur=2*n*M
    best_a=a.copy(); best_cur=cur
    update_global(state,best_a,best_cur)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    temp_frac=0.25
    beta=0.1
    no=0; last_improve=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       it+=1
       if time.monotonic()-last_improve>stall_seconds:
           break
       c, M = conv_max(a); cur=2*n*M
       if cur < best_cur: best...
       if M<=0: break
       p = peak_weights(c,M,temp_frac)
       g = shift_corr(p,a)
       if g.max()<=0: break
       d = g - g.mean()
       sc = max(abs(d))+1e-300
       d = d/sc
       moved=False
       # candidate beta values: use current beta scaled
       betas = [beta, beta*2, beta*0.5, beta*0.25, beta*4, beta*0.125]
       # sort by closeness to previous? maybe unique
       for b in betas:
          if b<=0.001 or b>1.0: continue
          cand = a - b*d
          cand = normalize(cand)
          if cand.sum? 
          s = score_only(cand)
          if s < cur - 1e-12:
             a=cand; cur=s; moved=True; no=0; last_improve=...
             # update beta: if b < beta maybe beta=b*1.5? Use beta = b if b <= beta else b*0.8
             if b <= beta: beta = min(0.5, b*1.5)
             else: beta = max(0.01, b*0.8)
             temp_frac = max(0.06, temp_frac*0.95)
             break
       if not moved:
          # multiplicative
          for b in betas:
             rate=np.clip(d,-2,2)
             cand=normalize(a*np.exp(-b*rate))
             s=score_only(cand)
             if s < cur -1e-12:
                a=cand; cur=s; moved=True; no=0; last_improve=...
                beta = ...; temp_frac=...
                break
       if not moved:
          no +=1
          if no % 10 ==0:
             temp_frac = 0.35 if no%20==0 else max(0.5, temp_frac*1.2)
          if no > 20:
             sigma = 0.03 + 0.02*min(5,no//10)
             a = random_perturb(a,rng,sigma)
             no=0
             # recompute cur after perturb? optional
    # final update
    c,M=conv_max(a); cur=2*n*M; update; return a
```

Implement `lp_direction(f, M, rows)`:
```
n=len(f)
A=np.zeros((len(rows), n))
for r,k in enumerate(rows):
   k=int(k)
   jlo=max(0,k-n+1); jhi=min(n,k+1)
   A[r, jlo:jhi]=f[k - (jlo + np.arange(jhi-jlo))]
cvec=-np.ones(n)
bvec=np.ones(len(rows))
res=linprog(cvec,A_ub=A,b_ub=bvec,bounds=(0,None),method='highs')
if res.status==0 and res.x.sum()>1: return normalize(res.x)
else None
```

Can we construct a sequence using a simple iterative algorithm that converges to an explicit optimum offline in code within runtime? Maybe use the recurrence for fixed max M to maximize sum over all coefficients (both early/late). We can solve for a sequence by iterative "min-max equalization":
Given target score T, M=T/(2n). Need find a sum1 with all c≤M. We can perform bisection on M and an iterative algorithm to maximize sum subject to c≤M. For fixed M, this is like finding a polynomial with coefficients nonnegative and square bounded. There is a known "Newton-like" method: choose a0=√M, then choose a_k to make c_k as close to M as possible, also future? Could use both early and late by solving linear equations? For symmetric target, maybe we can design by making c_k constant for all k via choosing a0 and a_{n-1}. For full constant coefficients M (all 2n-1), recurrence from both ends? Since c0=a0^2=M, c_{2n-2}=a_{n-1}^2=M. Then c_{n-1} includes 2a0a_{n-1} plus middle; if a0=a_{n-1}=√M, 2M already >M impossible. Thus central can't be constant if endpoints large; max must be higher. But optimal maybe has max at endpoints and central equal? For score 1.5, M=0.75/n. Endpoints a0^2≤M => a0≤0.0866/√n. Sum of n with max each 0.0866/√n at endpoints only? To sum1, need many entries; if all entries ≤0.0866/√n, sum≤0.0866√n. For n=512, 0.0866*22.6=1.96>1 possible. So a_i can be O(1/√n). The binomial has a0=1/√(π n)? `conv_max(a)`:
```
a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
n = a.size
if n==0: return np.array([0.0]),0.0
a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
s = a.sum()
if s <= 1e-300: return np.zeros(2*n-1),0.0
if abs(s-1.0) > 1e-12: a = a/s
size = 1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
fa=np.zeros(size); fa[:n]=a
F=np.fft.rfft(fa)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]
c = np.where(c>0, c, 0.0)
return c, float(c.max())
```
For n=1, 2n-2=0 bit_length? 0.bit_length()=0; size=1. c length1. Good.

`score_only(a)` uses conv_max after normalization? conv_max normalizes if sum not1. But it clips; sum maybe not1. Use conv_max.

pb=np.zeros(size); pb[:m]=p[::-1]
out=np.fft.irfft(...)
idx=(m-1)-np.arange(n)
res=out[idx]
res=np.where(res>0,res,0)
return res
```

`peak_weights(c,M,temp_frac)`:
```
delta = max(1e-300, temp_frac*M)
w = c - (M - delta)
w = np.where(w>0, w, 0.0)
s=w.sum()
if not finite or s<=0:
   w=zeros; w[argmax(c)]=1
else: w/=s
return w
```

`make_initials`:
```
def make_initials(n, rng, count=16):
    seqs=[]
    base=gen_binomial(n,0.5)
    seqs.append(base)
    seqs.append(base[::-1].copy())
    for alpha in (0.45,0.55,0.6,0.7): seqs.append(gen_binomial(n,alpha))
    idx=np.arange(n)+1.0
    t=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    frac=t/(n-1 if n>1 else 1)
    for r in (-0.3,0.0,0.3,0.7,1.2):
       y=base*np.power((idx)/n, r)
       seqs.append(normalize(y))
    # beta
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    for a_exp in (-0.5,-0.3,-0.1,0.1,0.4):
       for b_exp in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
           y=np.power(x,a_exp)*np.power(1-x+1e-12,b_exp)
           seqs.append(normalize(y))
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(4):
       z=rng.normal(0,1,n)
       wlen=max(1,min(n,1+n//50))
       if wlen>1: w=np.ones(wlen); w/=w.sum(); z=np.convolve(z,w,mode='same')
       y=base+0.3*np.exp(z)
       seqs.append(normalize(y))
    # symmetric low
    seqs.append(normalize(0.5*(base+base[::-1])))
    # right bump
    center=n*0.75; width=max(1,n//8); y=np.exp(-((np.arange(n)-center)/width)**2); seqs.append(normalize(base*0.5 + y))
    return seqs
```
Too many (maybe 25). For n=2048, scoring 25*FFT 4096 okay. But local top K, not all. We can limit by taking all scored; update global for all (maybe good). Fine.

For beta exp negative, x small but >0; okay.

Need ensure no complex from power with negative base; x positive, 1-x positive; for x=0? using (i+0.5)/n >0 and <1 if i<n-1? For i=n-1, x=(n-0.5)/n=1-0.5/n, positive. Good.

`gen_binomial` for alpha<1 loops O(n), fine.

`resize_sequence`:
```
def resize_sequence(a,N):
 a=normalize(a)
 if a.size==N return a
 if a.size<N:
   x=np.linspace(0, a.size-1, N)
   out=np.interp(x, np.arange(a.size), a)
   return normalize(np.maximum(out,0))
 else:
   out=np.zeros(N)
   for i in range(N):
     lo=int(i*a.size//N); hi=int((i+1)*a.size//N)
     if hi>lo: out[i]=a[lo:hi].sum()
   return normalize(out)
```
Loop for downsample; N up to 4096, okay. Could vectorize with np.add.reduceat but loop fine.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0,budget_s-80)
    state={'score':inf,'seq':None}
    update_global(state, np.array([1/3,2/3]),16/9)
    prov=load_provided_sequence()
    tasks=[]
    if prov is not None:
       sc=score_only(prov); update
       tasks.append((sc, prov.size, prov, 150.0))
       for sig in (0.05,0.15): tasks.append((sc, prov.size, random_perturb(prov,rng,sig), 60.0))
    # stage1
    for n in (128,256,512,1024,2048):
       if time>deadline: break
       seqs=make_initials(n,rng)
       scored=[]
       for s in seqs:
          sc=score_only(s); update
          scored.append((sc,s))
       scored.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
       K=3 if n<=256 else 2 if n<=1024 else 1
       base_time={128:15,256:30,512:80,1024:100,2048:90}.get(n,60)
       for rank,(sc,s) in enumerate(scored[:K]):
          task_time=base_time if rank==0 else base_time*0.5
          tasks.append((sc,s,task_time))
    # sort by score, then longer n? Use key score and negative? itemgetter(0,2) where 2 is seq length in tuple.
    tasks.sort(key=itemgetter(0,1)) # score, seq length
    # But tasks tuple 3; itemgetter(0,1) works.
    # Run tasks
    for sc,seq,t in tasks:
       if time+10>deadline: break
       limit=min(t, deadline-time)
       if limit<5: continue
       local_search(seq, limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=20 if seq.size>1024 else 25)
       if state['score']<1.5:
          # maybe continue fewer
       if len(tasks)>? 
    # stage2: upscale best
    if state['seq'] is not None:
      for target_n in (512,1024,2048,4096):
         if target_n <= state['seq'].size: continue
         if time+40>deadline: break
         seq=resize_sequence(state['seq'], target_n)
         limit=min(60, deadline-time)
         local_search(seq, limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=20)
      # perturb best same n
      for sigma in (0.1,0.25,0.5):
         if time+30>deadline: break
         seq=random_perturb(state['seq'], rng, sigma)
         local_search(seq, min(40, deadline-time), rng, state, stall_seconds=20)
    # final refine current best
    if state['seq'] is not None and time+30<deadline:
       local_search(state['seq'].copy(), min(60, deadline-time), rng, state, stall_seconds=30)
    # ensure return valid
    if state['seq'] is None: cand=[1/3,2/3]
    else: cand=[float(x) for x in np.clip(state['seq'],0,1000)]
    # optional evaluate_sequence
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence(cand)
       print('final',val,len(cand))
       if not np.isfinite(val): cand=[1/3,2/3]
    except Exception: pass
    return cand
```

Detailed local_search code:
```
def local_search(a0, time_limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=25):
    a=normalize(a0)
    n=a.size
    if n<2 or time_limit<=0: return a
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0.0,float(time_limit))
    c,M=conv_max(a)
    cur=2.0*n*M
    best_a=a.copy(); best_cur=cur
    update_global(state,best_a,best_cur)
    last_imp=time.monotonic()
    no=0; it=0
    temp_frac=0.3
    beta=0.2
    lp_count=0
    max_lp = 40 if n<=512 else 15 if n<=1024 else 0
    lp_mod = 30 if n<=512 else 60
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       if time.monotonic()-last_imp > stall_seconds: break
       it+=1
       c,M=conv_max(a); cur=2*n*M
       if cur<best_cur:
          best_cur=cur; best_a=a.copy(); last_imp=time.monotonic(); no=0
          update_global(state,best_a,best_cur)
       if M<=1e-300: break
       p=peak_weights(c,M,temp_frac)
       g=shift_correlation(p,a)
       if g.max()<=1e-300:
          no +=1
          a=random_perturb(a,rng,0.1)
          continue
       d=g - np.mean(g)
       dscale=np.max(np.abs(d))
       if dscale<=1e-300:
          no +=1; a=random_perturb(a,rng,0.05); continue
       d=d/dscale
       moved=False
       # beta candidates unique
       betas=[beta, beta*2.0, beta*0.5, beta*0.2, beta*4.0, beta*0.1]
       # sort by proximity? just list
       # multiplicative primary
       for b in betas:
          if b<0.003 or b>1.5: continue
          arg=-b*d
          arg=np.clip(arg,-3.0,3.0)
          cand=a*np.exp(arg)
          cand=normalize(cand)
          if cand.size==0: continue
          s=score_only(cand)
          if np.isfinite(s) and s < cur - 1e-12:
             a=cand; cur=s; moved=True; no=0; last_imp=time.monotonic()
             if b <= beta: beta = min(0.8, b*1.5)
             else: beta = max(0.02, b*0.7)
             temp_frac = max(0.05, temp_frac*0.95)
             break
       if not moved:
          # additive
          for b in betas:
             if b<0.003 or b>1.0: continue
             cand = a - b*d
             cand=normalize(cand)
             s=score_only(cand)
             if np.isfinite(s) and s < cur -1e-12:
                a=cand; cur=s; moved=True; no=0; last_imp=...
                beta=...
                break
       if moved:
          continue
       # no move
       no +=1
       if no % 8 ==0:
          temp_frac = 0.35 if no % 16 ==0 else min(0.7, temp_frac*1.25)
       # LP attempt
       if max_lp>0 and lp_count<max_lp and (no >= 10 and it % lp_mod ==0):
          gdir=lp_direction(a,c,M)
          lp_count +=1
          if gdir is not None:
             for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0):
                cand=normalize((1.0-t)*a + t*gdir)
                s=score_only(cand)
                if s < cur -1e-12:
                   a=cand; cur=s; moved=True; no=0; last_imp=...
                   break
             if moved:
                temp_frac=max(0.15,temp_frac*0.7)
                continue
       if not moved:
          if no > 20:
             sigma=0.05 + 0.03*min(4,no//10)
             a=random_perturb(a,rng,sigma)
             no=0
             # optionally update cur?
    c,M=conv_max(a); cur=2*n*M
    if cur<best_cur: update
    else: update with best? ensure final best updated
    return best_a
```

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative step-function height sequence minimizing

    2 * n * max_k (a * a)[k] / (sum a)^2

where * denotes linear convolution.

The implementation normalizes sequences to sum one and optimizes the
scale-invariant score 2*n*max(a*a).  It uses a portfolio of strong
initializations:

  * truncated binomial / power-series profiles (alpha near 1/2),
  * beta/power-law profiles,
  * smooth random perturbations,
  * symmetrized and endpoint-bump variants,
  * the previously provided construction, if available.

Local refinement combines:
  * smooth-max subgradient steps with adaptive multiplicative/additive
    line search,
  * occasional LP directions on active convolution constraints,
  * random escape perturbations.

All routines are top-level; no lambdas or closures are used.
"""

import time

import numpy as np
from operator import itemgetter

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def next_power_of_two(m: int) -> int:
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << max(1, (m - 1).bit_length())


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def convolution_full(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    fa[:n] = a
    f = np.fft.rfft(fa)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, size)[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def convolution_max(a):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    c = convolution_full(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c, 0.0
    return c, float(c.max())


def score_only(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    c = convolution_full(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def shift_correlation(p, f):
    """
    Return r[j] = sum_i f[i] * p[i+j] for j=0..n-1.
    This is the derivative kernel needed for self-convolution gradients.
    """
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    m = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    need = n + m - 1
    size = next_power_of_two(need)
    fa = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    fa[:n] = f
    pb = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    pb[:m] = p[::-1]
    out = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(fa, size) * np.fft.rfft(pb, size), size)
    idx = (m - 1) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    r = out[idx]
    r = np.asarray(r, dtype=np.float64)
    r = np.where(np.isfinite(r), r, 0.0)
    r = np.maximum(r, 0.0)
    return r


def peak_weights(c, M, temp_frac):
    """Tempered weights for the largest convolution coefficients."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(M)
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w

    delta = max(1e-300, float(temp_frac) * M)
    floor = M - delta
    w = c - floor
    w = np.where(w > 0.0, w, 0.0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        k = int(np.argmax(c))
        w2 = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w2[k] = 1.0
        return w2
    return w / s


def gen_binomial_power(n, alpha):
    """
    Generate coefficients (alpha)_i / i! (truncated), normalized and
    nonnegative.  For alpha = 0.5 this is the central binomial profile.
    """
    n = int(max(1, n))
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * (i + alpha - 1.0) / float(i)
    return normalize_nonneg(a)


def gen_beta_power(n, a_exp, b_exp):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    x = np.clip(x, 1e-12, 1.0 - 1e-12)
    y = np.power(x, float(a_exp)) * np.power(1.0 - x, float(b_exp))
    return normalize_nonneg(y)


def smooth_noise(n, rng):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    z = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
    wlen = max(1, min(n, 1 + max(1, n // 50)))
    if wlen > 1:
        w = np.ones(wlen, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= w.sum()
        z = np.convolve(z, w, mode="same")
    return z


def make_initials(n, rng):
    """Create a diverse but strong initial portfolio at length n."""
    n = int(max(4, n))
    seqs = []

    base = gen_binomial_power(n, 0.5)
    seqs.append(base)
    seqs.append(base[::-1].copy())

    # Slightly different power-series profiles.
    for alpha in (0.42, 0.47, 0.53, 0.58, 0.65, 0.75):
        seqs.append(gen_binomial_power(n, alpha))

    # Shape the binomial profile toward the left or right.
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    frac = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    for r in (-0.35, -0.15, 0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 1.0, 1.8):
        y = base * np.power(idx / float(n), r)
        seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(y))

    # Beta / polynomial endpoint profiles.
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    x = np.clip(x, 1e-12, 1.0 - 1e-12)
    for ae in (-0.5, -0.35, -0.2, -0.05, 0.0, 0.15, 0.35):
        for be in (0.0, 0.3, 0.7, 1.3):
            y = np.power(x, ae) * np.power(1.0 - x, be)
            seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(y))

    # Smooth random perturbations of the binomial profile.
    for _ in range(5):
        z = smooth_noise(n, rng)
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        y = base * (1.0 + amp * np.tanh(z)) + phase * np.exp(z)
        seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(y))

    # Symmetrized endpoint profile.  Often a worse raw start, but a useful basin.
    seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(0.5 * (base + base[::-1])))

    # Binomial plus a smooth bump in the right half.
    centers = (0.55, 0.7, 0.82)
    for cfac in centers:
        center = cfac * n
        width = max(2.0, n * 0.08)
        y = base * 0.6 + np.exp(-((idx - center) / width) ** 2)
        seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(y))

    # Very broad random exponentiated spline.
    for _ in range(3):
        c = smooth_noise(n, rng)
        y = np.exp(0.9 * c)
        seqs.append(normalize_nonneg(y))

    return seqs


def load_provided_sequence():
    try:
        raw = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        return None
    try:
        a = np.asarray(raw, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a is None or a.size == 0:
        return None
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if float(a.sum()) <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return normalize_nonneg(a)


def resize_sequence(a, N):
    """Resize a nonnegative sequence to exact length N."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    N = int(max(1, N))
    m = a.size
    if m == N:
        return a
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(N, 1.0 / float(N), dtype=np.float64)

    if m < N:
        old = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
        new = np.linspace(0.0, float(m - 1), N)
        out = np.interp(new, old, a)
        out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
        return normalize_nonneg(out)

    out = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(N):
        lo = int((i * m) // N)
        hi = int(((i + 1) * m) // N)
        if hi > lo:
            out[i] = float(a[lo:hi].sum())
    return normalize_nonneg(out)


def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return a
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=n)
    b = a * np.exp(noise)
    b = normalize_nonneg(b)
    return b


def update_global(state, seq, score):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return False
    seq = normalize_nonneg(seq)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return False
    old = float(state["score"])
    if score < old - 1e-12:
        state["score"] = float(score)
        state["seq"] = np.array(seq, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
        if not np.isfinite(old) or score < old - 1e-4:
            print("update_best score=%.12f n=%d" % (float(score), int(seq.size)), flush=True)
        return True
    return False


def build_lp_matrix(f, rows):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    rows = np.asarray(rows, dtype=np.int64)
    A = np.zeros((rows.size, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for r, k in enumerate(rows):
        k = int(k)
        if k < 0 or k >= 2 * n - 1:
            continue
        jlo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        jhi = min(n, k + 1)
        if jhi > jlo:
            col = jlo + np.arange(jhi - jlo, dtype=np.int64)
            A[r, col] = f[k - col]
    return A


def lp_direction(f, c, M):
    """
    Solves max sum(g) s.t. (f*g)_k <= M for selected active rows,
    0 <= g_j <= U.  Returns a nonnegative g direction, or None.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return None
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    if n < 2 or n > 1536:
        return None
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
        return None

    m = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= 512:
        rows = np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        k = min(700, m)
        if k >= m:
            top = np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
        else:
            top = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:].astype(np.int64)
        extra = np.arange(min(250, n), dtype=np.int64)
        rows = np.unique(np.concatenate((top, extra)))

    if rows.size == 0:
        return None

    A = build_lp_matrix(f, rows)
    if A.size == 0 or not np.any(A > 0.0):
        return None

    cvec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bvec = np.ones(rows.size, dtype=np.float64)
    U = 12.0
    bounds = [(0.0, U)] * n

    try:
        res = linprog(
            cvec,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=bvec,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": "high"},
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is not None and getattr(res, "status", None) == 0:
        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        s = float(g.sum())
        if np.isfinite(s) and s > 1.0 + 1e-6:
            return g
    return None


def local_search(a0, time_limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=25.0):
    """
    Local projected subgradient search with adaptive step sizes,
    occasional LP directions, and random escapes.
    """
    a = normalize_nonneg(a0)
    n = a.size
    time_limit = float(max(0.0, time_limit))
    if n < 2 or time_limit < 1.0:
        return a

    deadline = time.monotonic() + time_limit

    c, M = convolution_max(a)
    cur = float(2.0 * n * M)
    best_a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
    best_cur = cur
    update_global(state, best_a, best_cur)

    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    temp_frac = 0.30
    beta = 0.15

    if n <= 512:
        max_lp = 6
        lp_mod = 50
    elif n <= 1024:
        max_lp = 3
        lp_mod = 100
    else:
        max_lp = 0
        lp_mod = 10 ** 9
    lp_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > float(stall_seconds):
            break

        it += 1
        c, M = convolution_max(a)
        cur = float(2.0 * n * M)

        if np.isfinite(cur) and cur < best_cur - 1e-12:
            best_cur = cur
            best_a = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            no_improve = 0
            update_global(state, best_a, best_cur)

        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
            break

        p = peak_weights(c, M, temp_frac)
        g = shift_correlation(p, a)
        if g.size == 0 or float(g.max()) <= 1e-300:
            no_improve += 1
            a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.08)
            continue

        d = g - float(np.mean(g))
        dscale = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
        if not np.isfinite(dscale) or dscale <= 1e-300:
            no_improve += 1
            a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.05)
            continue
        d = d / dscale

        moved = False

        if n <= 1024:
            betas = [beta, beta * 2.0, beta * 0.5, beta * 4.0, beta * 0.2]
        else:
            betas = [beta, beta * 2.0, beta * 0.5]

        # Multiplicative positive-safe update.
        for b in betas:
            if b < 0.003 or b > 1.5:
                continue
            arg = -b * d
            arg = np.clip(arg, -3.0, 3.0)
            cand = a * np.exp(arg)
            cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
            if cand.size != n or float(cand.max()) <= 0.0:
                continue
            s = score_only(cand)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < cur - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                cur = s
                moved = True
                no_improve = 0
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                if b <= beta:
                    beta = min(0.80, b * 1.5)
                else:
                    beta = max(0.02, b * 0.7)
                temp_frac = max(0.05, temp_frac * 0.95)
                break

        # Additive tangent-space update.
        if not moved:
            for b in betas:
                if b < 0.003 or b > 1.0:
                    continue
                cand = a - b * d
                cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
                if cand.size != n or float(cand.max()) <= 0.0:
                    continue
                s = score_only(cand)
                if np.isfinite(s) and s < cur - 1e-12:
                    a = cand
                    cur = s
                    moved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    last_improve = time.monotonic()
                    if b <= beta:
                        beta = min(0.60, b * 1.4)
                    else:
                        beta = max(0.02, b * 0.8)
                    temp_frac = max(0.06, temp_frac * 0.97)
                    break

        if moved:
            continue

        no_improve += 1

        # Periodically widen the smooth-max weight to escape.
        if no_improve % 8 == 0:
            if no_improve % 16 == 0:
                temp_frac = 0.38
            else:
                temp_frac = min(0.75, temp_frac * 1.25)

        # Occasional LP direction.
        if (
            max_lp > 0
            and lp_count < max_lp
            and no_improve >= 10
            and (it % lp_mod == 0)
        ):
            gdir = lp_direction(a, c, M)
            lp_count += 1
            if gdir is not None:
                gdir = normalize_nonneg(gdir)
                for t in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
                    cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * gdir
                    cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
                    s = score_only(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(s) and s < cur - 1e-12:
                        a = cand
                        cur = s
                        moved = True
                        no_improve = 0
                        last_improve = time.monotonic()
                        temp_frac = max(0.12, temp_frac * 0.65)
                        break
                if moved:
                    continue

        # Random escape.
        if no_improve > 20:
            sigma = 0.05 + 0.03 * min(4.0, float(no_improve // 10))
            a = random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)
            no_improve = 0

    c, M = convolution_max(best_a)
    final = float(2.0 * best_a.size * M)
    update_global(state, best_a, final)
    return best_a


def safe_final_sequence(state):
    if state is None or state.get("seq") is None:
        return [float(1.0 / 3.0), float(2.0 / 3.0)]
    a = np.asarray(state["seq"], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if a.size == 0 or s <= 1e-8:
        return [float(0.5), float(0.5)]
    a = a / s
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    return [float(x) for x in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point invoked by the evaluator.

    Runs a time-bounded portfolio search and returns the best
    nonnegative sequence found as a Python list of floats.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(max(120.0, float(budget_s)))
    deadline = t0 + max(120.0, budget - 90.0)

    state = {"score": float("inf"), "seq": None}

    # Guaranteed valid fallbacks.
    update_global(state, np.array([1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0], dtype=np.float64), 16.0 / 9.0)
    update_global(state, np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64), score_only(np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64)))

    tasks = []

    # Use the previously provided construction, if present.
    prov = load_provided_sequence()
    if prov is not None:
        prov_n = prov.size
        prov_score = score_only(prov)
        update_global(state, prov, prov_score)

        work_n = min(prov_n, 4096)
        work = resize_sequence(prov, work_n)
        work_score = score_only(work)
        update_global(state, work, work_score)

        tasks.append((work_score, work_n, work, 170.0))
        for sigma in (0.05, 0.12, 0.28):
            pert = random_perturb(work, rng, sigma)
            sp = score_only(pert)
            tasks.append((sp, pert.size, pert, 55.0))

    # Portfolio over useful lengths.
    base_times = {64: 8.0, 128: 16.0, 256: 34.0, 512: 95.0, 1024: 120.0, 2048: 105.0}
    for n in (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048):
        if time.monotonic() + 5.0 > deadline:
            break

        seqs = make_initials(n, rng)
        scored = []
        for s in seqs:
            sc = score_only(s)
            update_global(state, s, sc)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, s))

        scored.sort(key=itemgetter(0))

        if n <= 256:
            kbest = 3
        elif n <= 1024:
            kbest = 3
        else:
            kbest = 2

        for rank in range(min(kbest, len(scored))):
            sc, s = scored[rank]
            tt = base_times.get(n, 60.0)
            if rank == 0:
                tt *= 1.0
            elif rank == 1:
                tt *= 0.60
            else:
                tt *= 0.35
            tasks.append((sc, s.size, s, tt))

    # Sort by initial score, then by length (larger length gets tie priority).
    tasks.sort(key=itemgetter(0, 1))

    # Run task list until time nearly expires.
    for item in tasks:
        if time.monotonic() + 8.0 > deadline:
            break
        _, seq_len, seq, tt = item
        if seq_len < 2:
            continue
        limit = min(float(tt), max(5.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if limit < 5.0:
            continue
        stall = 20.0 if seq_len > 1024 else 25.0
        local_search(seq, limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=stall)

    # Upscale the current best to larger grids for further refinement.
    if state["seq"] is not None:
        cur_n = int(state["seq"].size)
        for target_n in (512, 1024, 2048, 4096):
            if time.monotonic() + 25.0 > deadline:
                break
            if target_n <= cur_n:
                continue
            limit = min(75.0, max(12.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
            if limit < 12.0:
                continue
            seq = resize_sequence(state["seq"], target_n)
            local_search(seq, limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=20.0)
            if state["seq"] is not None:
                cur_n = int(state["seq"].size)

        # Refine noisy versions of the current best.
        for sigma in (0.10, 0.22, 0.45):
            if time.monotonic() + 20.0 > deadline:
                break
            seq = random_perturb(state["seq"], rng, sigma)
            limit = min(45.0, max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
            if limit < 10.0:
                continue
            local_search(seq, limit, rng, state, stall_seconds=20.0)

        # Final polish on the current best itself.
        if time.monotonic() + 25.0 < deadline:
            limit = min(80.0, max(15.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
            local_search(
                resize_sequence(state["seq"], int(state["seq"].size)),
                limit,
                rng,
                state,
                stall_seconds=30.0,
            )

    cand = safe_final_sequence(state)

    try:
        official = evaluate_sequence(cand)
        if np.isfinite(official):
            print("final_official score=%.12f n=%d" % (float(official), len(cand)), flush=True)
        else:
            cand = safe_final_sequence(state)
    except Exception:
        pass

    return cand
```
